=== PROMPT VARIANT ===

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an integer power and two integer arrays damage and health, both having length n.
Bob has n enemies, where enemy i will deal Bob damage[i] points of damage per second while they are alive (i.e. health[i] > 0).
Every second, after the enemies deal damage to Bob, he chooses one of the enemies that is still alive and deals power points of damage to them.
Determine the minimum total amount of damage points that will be dealt to Bob before all n enemies are dead.
 
Example 1:

Input: power = 4, damage = [1,2,3,4], health = [4,5,6,8]
Output: 39
Explanation:

Attack enemy 3 in the first two seconds, after which enemy 3 will go down, the number of damage points dealt to Bob is 10 + 10 = 20 points.
Attack enemy 2 in the next two seconds, after which enemy 2 will go down, the number of damage points dealt to Bob is 6 + 6 = 12 points.
Attack enemy 0 in the next second, after which enemy 0 will go down, the number of damage points dealt to Bob is 3 points.
Attack enemy 1 in the next two seconds, after which enemy 1 will go down, the number of damage points dealt to Bob is 2 + 2 = 4 points.


Example 2:

Input: power = 1, damage = [1,1,1,1], health = [1,2,3,4]
Output: 20
Explanation:

Attack enemy 0 in the first second, after which enemy 0 will go down, the number of damage points dealt to Bob is 4 points.
Attack enemy 1 in the next two seconds, after which enemy 1 will go down, the number of damage points dealt to Bob is 3 + 3 = 6 points.
Attack enemy 2 in the next three seconds, after which enemy 2 will go down, the number of damage points dealt to Bob is 2 + 2 + 2 = 6 points.
Attack enemy 3 in the next four seconds, after which enemy 3 will go down, the number of damage points dealt to Bob is 1 + 1 + 1 + 1 = 4 points.


Example 3:

Input: power = 8, damage = [40], health = [59]
Output: 320

 
Constraints:

1 <= power <= 10^4
1 <= n == damage.length == health.length <= 10^5
1 <= damage[i], health[i] <= 10^4

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def minDamage(self, power: int, damage: List[int], health: List[int]) -> int:
        
```

- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's prove greedy via adjacent exchange. Suppose two enemies A and B with p_A, d_A, p_B, d_B. If A immediately before B, total damage contribution from these two over their intervals (starting at time S before A) is:
A before B: d_A(S+p_A) + d_B(S+p_A+p_B) = (d_A+d_B)S + d_A p_A + d_B p_A + d_B p_B.
B before A: d_B(S+p_B)+d_A(S+p_B+p_A) = (d_A+d_B)S + d_B p_B + d_A p_B + d_A p_A.
Difference A before B - B before A = d_B p_A - d_A p_B. So A before B better iff d_B p_A < d_A p_B => d_A/p_A > d_B/p_B. Thus sort descending ratio. This assumes no preemption and jobs contiguous. Now code.

Option 1: Use list of tuples `(damage[i], t_i)` and sort with `cmp_to_key`.

```python
from typing import List
from functools import cmp_to_key

class Solution:
    def minDamage(self, power: int, damage: List[int], health: List[int]) -> int:
        enemies = []
        for d, h in zip(damage, health):
            t = (h + power - 1) // power
            enemies.append((d, t))
        
        def cmp(a, b):
            # a before b if a.damage / a.time > b.damage / b.time
            # return negative if a should come before b
            lhs = a[0] * b[1]
            rhs = b[0] * a[1]
            if lhs > rhs:
                return -1
            if lhs < rhs:
                return 1
            return 0
        
        enemies.sort(key=cmp_to_key(cmp))
        ans = 0
        time = 0
        for d, t in enemies:
            time += t
            ans += d * time
        return ans
```

Need be careful comparator semantics: cmp_to_key expects negative if first < second. We want descending ratio. If a before b better when d_a * t_b > d_b * t_a. So if lhs > rhs, return -1 (a considered less). If lhs < rhs return 1. Good.

Could also use float key:

```python
enemies = [(d, (h+power-1)//power) for d,h in zip(damage, health)]
enemies.sort(key=lambda x: x[0] / x[1], reverse=True)
```

So safe by factor 1e4. Even if ratio near 0? Min positive ratio 1/10000 = 1e-4, absolute precision ~1e-20, diff min 1e-8? Potential issue with `cmp_to_key` and `List` import. Code:

```python
from typing import List
from functools import cmp_to_key

class Solution:
    def minDamage(self, power: int, damage: List[int], health: List[int]) -> int:
        enemies = []
        for d, h in zip(damage, health):
            attacks = (h + power - 1) // power
            enemies.append((d, attacks))
        
        def compare(a, b):
            left = a[0] * b[1]
            right = b[0] * a[1]
            if left > right:
                return -1
            if left < right:
                return 1
            return 0
        
        enemies.sort(key=cmp_to_key(compare))
        
        total_time = 0
        ans = 0
        for d, attacks in enemies:
            total_time += attacks
            ans += d * total_time
        return ans
```

Wait comparator for descending ratio: If a ratio > b ratio, a should come before b. In Python sort, comparator returns negative if a < b. So return -1. Good.

Now, let's think about "preemption not useful" proof more: In a preemptive schedule, let jobs complete in order π. For each job π_k, its completion time C_{π_k} is at least sum_{r=1}^k p_{π_r} because by that time the machine must have completed all those jobs; total processing of those jobs is sum. It may have also processed jobs that complete later, so C is larger. If we schedule jobs nonpreemptively in order π, job π_k completes at exactly sum_{r=1}^k p_{π_r} <= C_{π_k}. Thus weighted sum no larger. Therefore there exists optimal nonpreemptive schedule. Then WSPT. Good.

If I use comparator, code:

```python
from typing import List
from functools import cmp_to_key

class Solution:
    def minDamage(self, power: int, damage: List[int], health: List[int]) -> int:
        enemies = []
        for d, h in zip(damage, health):
            enemies.append((d, (h + power - 1) // power))
        
        def cmp(a, b):
            lhs = a[0] * b[1]
            rhs = b[0] * a[1]
            if lhs > rhs:
                return -1
            if lhs < rhs:
                return 1
            return 0
        
        enemies.sort(key=cmp_to_key(cmp))
        
        ans = 0
        elapsed = 0
        for d, t in enemies:
            elapsed += t
            ans += d * elapsed
        return ans
```

This is clear and exact. Potential issue: `cmp_to_key` comparator function named `cmp` shadows built-in? No built-in `cmp` in Python 3. Fine.

If I use float key, code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minDamage(...):
        enemies = [(d, (h + power - 1)//power) for d,h in zip(damage, health)]
        enemies.sort(key=lambda x: x[0] / x[1], reverse=True)
        ...
```

This is shorter and faster. But if someone argues floating point, not ideal. Since constraints safe, but exact comparator is more rigorous. I'll use comparator.

Let's think about if `cmp_to_key` with comparator returning 0 for equal ratios but sort stable; if equal ratios any order. Good.

But LeetCode Python time limit usually not too tight. The problem constraints 1e5, O(n log n) with comparator should pass. Let's maybe estimate more accurately: CPython 3.10, `cmp_to_key` sort of 100k random ints? I recall `cmp_to_key` can be ~10x slower than key sort. Key sort 100k ~0.02s? Actually Python sort 100k floats ~0.02-0.05s. cmp_to_key maybe 0.3-0.5s. Still fine. If time limit 2s, okay. If 1s, maybe okay. PyPy cmp_to_key maybe slower? But still 1.7M comparisons, okay. Let's not worry.

```python
enemies = [(d, (h + power - 1) // power) for d, h in zip(damage, health)]
```

This is fine. Use loop or comprehension. Comprehension faster. Let's use comprehension.

Code:

```python
from typing import List
from functools import cmp_to_key

class Solution:
    def minDamage(self, power: int, damage: List[int], health: List[int]) -> int:
        enemies = [
            (d, (h + power - 1) // power)
            for d, h in zip(damage, health)
        ]

        def cmp(a, b):
            lhs = a[0] * b[1]
            rhs = b[0] * a[1]
            if lhs > rhs:
                return -1
            if lhs < rhs:
                return 1
            return 0

        enemies.sort(key=cmp_to_key(cmp))

        ans = 0
        elapsed = 0
        for d, t in enemies:
            elapsed += t
            ans += d * elapsed
        return ans
```

This is final.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "left = a[0] * b[1]" where a[0] is damage, b[1] is attacks. This computes d_a * t_b. "right = b[0] * a[1]" d_b * t_a. If left > right, ratio a > b. Good.

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List
from functools import cmp_to_key

class Solution:
    def minDamage(self, power: int, damage: List[int], health: List[int]) -> int:
        enemies = [
            (d, (h + power - 1) // power)
            for d, h in zip(damage, health)
        ]

        def compare(a, b):
            # Sort by damage / attacks descending.
            left = a[0] * b[1]
            right = b[0] * a[1]
            if left > right:
                return -1
            if left < right:
                return 1
            return 0

        enemies.sort(key=cmp_to_key(compare))

        ans = 0
        elapsed = 0
        for d, attacks in enemies:
            elapsed += attacks
            ans += d * elapsed

        return ans
```
